\sum_{u\in R_{1}\cap V^{2b}}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot|N_{u}^{+}\cap C(u)\cap S_{d}|^{p-1}\cdot\sum_{w\in N_{u}^{+}\cap C(u)\cap S_{d}}y(u)^{p}

and

\sum_{u\in R_{1}\cap V^{2b}}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot|N_{u}^{+}\cap C(u)\cap S_{d}|^{p-1}\cdot\sum_{w\in N_{u}^{+}\cap C(u)\cap S_{d}}y(w)^{p}.

The first term is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in V_{0}:|C(u)|\leq 2\cdot|N_{u}^{+}|}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot|C(u)|^{p}\cdot y(u)^{p}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\cdot\sum_{u\in V}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{2p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{u\in V_{0}\}}\cdot y(u)^{p}.

Applying Proposition
1
, we obtain a bound of
\nicefrac{{664}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\sum_{u\in V}y(u)
in expectation for
p=1
, and of
2^{p}\cdot\left(\frac{C^{2}\cdot\log^{2}n}{\varepsilon^{4}}\right)^{p}\cdot\sum_{u\in V}y(u)^{p}
conditioned on the event
B^{c}
for finite
p\geq 1
. The second term is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\quad\sum_{w\in S_{d}}y(w)^{p}\sum_{u\in V_{0}\cap C(w):|C(u)|\leq 2\cdot|N_{u}^{+}|}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot|N_{u}^{+}\cap C(u)\cap S_{d}|^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{w\in S_{d}}y(w)^{p}\sum_{u\in V_{0}\cap C(w):|C(u)|\leq 2\cdot|N_{u}^{+}|}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot|C(w)|^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{w\in S_{d}}y(w)^{p}\sum_{u\in V_{0}\cap C(w):|C(w)|\leq 2\cdot|N_{u}^{+}|}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{2p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot\frac{|C(w)|^{p-1}}{|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{p}\cdot\sum_{w\in V}y(w)^{p}\sum_{u\in C(w)}\frac{|N_{u}^{+}|^{2p}}{|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|^{2p}}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{u\in V_{0}\}}\cdot\frac{|C(w)|^{p-1}}{|C(w)|^{p}}.

Again applying Proposition
1
, we obtain the same upper bounds as above for the first term.
∎

In total, we combine the results from Claims
21
,
22
, and
23
to see

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u\in V_{0}\cap R_{1}}|N_{u}^{+}|\right]\leq\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\right)\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}

(29)

and conditioning on
B^{c}
, for finite
p

\displaystyle\sum_{u\in V_{0}\cap R_{1}}|N_{u}^{+}|^{p}\leq O\left(\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\log^{2}n\right)^{p}\right)\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{p}^{p}.

(30)

Finally, for
u\in R_{2}
, we need to bound the contribution of
uv\in E^{+}
such that
v\in R_{1}
, i.e., of those
v
for which
\tilde{d}_{uv}=1\neq\bar{d}_{uv}
. We note that this case is not needed for
p=1
, since Claims
20
,
21
,
22
, and
23
suffice to compute the
\ell_{1}
-norm of the fractional cost.

Claim 24
.

Let
1\leq p<\infty
. Conditioned on the event
B_{c}
, we have

\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}|N_{u}^{+}\cap R_{1}\cap V_{0}|^{p}\leq O\left(\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}}\cdot\log^{3}n\right)^{p}\right)\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{p}^{p}.

Proof of Claim
24
.

Observe that

\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}|\{v:v\in N_{u}^{+}\cap R_{1}\cap V_{0}\mbox{ and }\bar{d}_{uv}\geq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}\}|^{p}\leq 4^{p}\cdot\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\left(\sum_{v\in V_{0}\cap N_{u}^{+}}\bar{d}_{uv}\right)^{p}.

By Claim
20
, this is bounded for
p=1
in expectation by
\frac{116}{\varepsilon^{2}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}
and for finite
p
by
\left(\nicefrac{{12C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\right)^{p}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{p}^{p}
, since we conditioned on
B^{c}
.

Given the above observation, with loss of a factor of
2^{p-1}
due to Jensen’s inequality, it suffices to bound the analogous quantity with
\bar{d}_{uv}\leq\nicefrac{{1}}{{4}}
. We create a bipartite auxiliary graph
H=(R_{2}\cap V_{0},R_{1}\cap V_{0},F)
. An edge
uv\in F
for
u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}
and
v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}
if
uv\in E^{+}
and
\bar{d}_{uv}\leq 1/4
. It then suffices to show

\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}\text{deg}_{H}(u)^{p}=O\left(\nicefrac{{\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\right)^{p}\cdot\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}|N_{v}^{+}|^{p},

since then we may bound the right-hand side via Claims
21
,
22
, and
23
(specifically, we combine them using Equation (
29
)). We will bound via double counting the quantity
\sum_{f=uv\in F}\left(\text{deg}_{H}(u)+\text{deg}_{H}(v)\right)^{p-1}
. Let
N_{H}(\cdot)
denote the neighborhoods in
H
of the vertices.

\displaystyle\sum_{f=uv\in F}\left(\text{deg}_{H}(u)+\text{deg}_{H}(v)\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{u\in N_{H}(v)}\left(\text{deg}_{H}(v)+\text{deg}_{H}(u)\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{u\in N_{H}(v)}\left(|N_{v}^{+}|+\nicefrac{{C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot|N_{u}^{+}\cap S_{d}|\right)^{p-1}

(31)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{u\in N_{H}(v)}\left(|N_{v}^{+}|+\nicefrac{{C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}\cdot|N_{v}^{+}\cap S_{d}|\right)^{p-1}

(32)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{u\in N_{H}(v)}(1+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}\cdot\nicefrac{{C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}})^{p-1}\cdot|N_{v}^{+}|^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\nicefrac{{4}}{{3}}\cdot\nicefrac{{C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}})^{p-1}\cdot\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}|N_{v}^{+}|\cdot|N_{v}^{+}|^{p-1}

\displaystyle\leq(3\cdot\nicefrac{{C\cdot\log n}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}})^{p-1}\cdot\sum_{v\in R_{1}\cap V_{0}}|N_{v}^{+}|^{p}

where (
31
) follows from Proposition
1
and (
32
) follows from Fact
4
. It now just remains to lower bound the sum:

\displaystyle\sum_{f=uv\in F}\left(\text{deg}_{H}(u)+\text{deg}_{H}(v)\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{v\in N_{H}(u)}\left(\text{deg}_{H}(u)+\text{deg}_{H}(v)\right)^{p-1}

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}\sum_{v\in N_{H}(u)}\text{deg}_{H}(u)^{p-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{u\in R_{2}\cap V_{0}}\text{deg}_{H}(u)^{p},

which is what we sought to show.
∎

Combining Claims
20
,
21
,
22
,
23
, and
24
completes the proof of Lemma
3
(using only the first four claims for
p=1
) with

\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\sum_{v\in N_{u}^{+}\cap V_{0}}\tilde{d}_{uv}\right]\leq\nicefrac{{1451}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}
, and conditioned on the event
B^{c}
,
\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\left(\sum_{v\in N_{u}^{+}\cap V_{0}}\tilde{d}_{uv}\right)^{p}\leq\left(\nicefrac{{110\cdot C^{3}\cdot\log^{3}n}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}}\right)^{p}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{p}^{p}.

∎

Appendix B
Tight analysis of Algorithm
1
for
p=1

While the proof of item
1
of Theorem
1
is valid when
p=1
, the analysis is rather lossy for this case. We know there exist
O(1)
algorithms for
\ell_{1}
-norm correlation clustering in the AOS model
[
22
]
, and we will show Algorithm
1
achieves this too.

As in the finite
\ell_{p}
-norm analysis (Section
5
), we partition our analysis into the cost of the disagreements incurred during the Pre-clustering phase (Section
B.1
), the cost of the disagreements incurred during the two individual Pivot phases (Section
B.2
), and those cost incurred between the Pre-clustering phase and the Pivot phases (Section
B.3
). See Figure
4
for references to the lemmas for each type of disagreement. Note there are fewer cases than in Figure
1
for the finite
\ell_{p}
-norm analysis, because we need not consider, e.g., whether
u\succ v
or
v\succ u
for the
\ell_{1}
-norm objective.

The analysis in Section
B.1
is for disagreements where both endpoints are eligible and at least one is pre-clustered (see the
blue
and
pink
edges in Figure
4
). The analysis here is similar to the corresponding analysis (Section
5.1
) for
\ell_{p}
-norms,
p\in(1,\infty)
, but involves more probabilistic subtleties in order to obtain an upper bound that both holds in expectation and nearly matches the lower bound.

The analysis in Sections
B.2
and
B.3
looks most dissimilar to the corresponding
\ell_{p}
-norm analyses (Sections
5.2
and
5.3
). For edges that have at least one endpoint in
\widebar{V_{0}}
(see the
orange
edges and
yellow
edges in Figure
4
), the key idea will be that the expected positive degree of a vertex in
G[\widebar{V_{0}}]
is small. This will help us charge the cost of disagreements to bad triangles (Definition
10
) and to the (true) correlation metric
d
(Definition
2
).

Finally, for the disagreements incurred from running Modified Pivot on
G[V_{0}^{\prime}]
, we will again charge to both bad triangles and to
\tilde{d}
, as we did for the
\ell_{p}
-norm analysis (Lemma
10
), but the analysis will be simpler as for the
\ell_{1}
-norm we may charge on a per-edge basis rather than a per-vertex basis.

Lemma
16

Lemma
17

Lemma
19

Lemma
20

Lemma
21

Figure 4:
An overview of the cost analysis of Algorithm
1
for
p=1
. As in Figure

, solid edges are disagreements and dashed ovals are clusters, and different types of disagreements are color coded with the corresponding lemmas where we handle their charging arguments.

B.1
Cost of Pre-clustering phase

In this section, we bound the cost of the pre-clustering phase in Algorithm
1
. By this we mean that we bound the cost of disagreeing edges that have at least one endpoint that is pre-clustered. We refer the reader to Section
5.1
for a reminder on notations and definitions related to the pre-clustering phase.

Define
t:=r/(2\delta)
to be a threshold parameter which will be used in our analysis.

B.1.1
Cost of positive edges

We begin in Lemma
16
by bounding the cost of positive edges that are disagreements in
\mathcal{C}_{\textsf{ALG}}
where both endpoints are eligible and at least one is pre-clustered. This lemma is an analog of Lemmas
6
/
7
in the cost analysis for
\ell_{p}
-norms,
p\in(1,\infty)
. Here, we need not consider separate lemmas because the
\ell_{1}
-norm objective is equivalent to minimizing the total number of edges that are disagreements.

Lemma 16
.

The expected cost of disagreements
uv\in E^{+}
where
u
and
v
are eligible and at least one is pre-clustered is

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|\{v\in N_{u}^{+}:v\succ u\}|\Big]\leq O\left(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}}\right)\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

Proof of Lemma
16
.

Observe first that each
v
in the statement of the lemma is in
V_{0}
, since
v\succ u
implies
v
is pre-clustered. As in Lemma
6
, for fixed
u\in V_{0}
partition the disagreements we wish to bound into two sets:

E_{1}(u):=\{v\in N_{u}^{+}:v\succ u\}\cap\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)

and

E_{2}(u):=\{v\in N_{u}^{+}:v\succ u\}\setminus E_{1}(u).

So we wish to bound

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|\{v\in N_{u}^{+}:v\succ u\}|\Big]=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|E_{2}(u)|\Big]

By Lemma
3
, the second term is at most

\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|E_{2}(u)|\Big]\leq\frac{1}{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\sum_{v\in N_{u}^{+}\cap V_{0}}\tilde{d}_{uv}\Big]\leq O(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}})\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

(33)

To bound the first term,
\mathbb{E}[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|E_{1}(u)|]
, we case on whether
u
has a close neighbor sampled by
S_{p}
. We note that the sums below are taken over
random
sets.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]}_{E_{1a}}+\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]}_{E_{1b}}

Figure 5:
Bounding
E_{1b}
in the proof of Lemma
16
. The orange points
v\in E_{1}(u)
are clustered before
u
, so edges
uv
are disagreements. To bound the cost of the orange points, it suffices to show that there are more purple points in expectation, and that the cost of the purple points can be charged to the fractional cost
\tilde{D}_{0}(u)
of
u
.

Bounding
E_{1a}
.

As in the proof of Lemma
6
, bounding
E_{1a}
is simpler because the expected size of
E_{1}(u)
must be small. We repeat the following claim, introduced in the proof of Lemma
6
, that shows there is sufficient fractional cost incident to
u
to which to charge
E_{1}(u)
.

Recall

\tilde{D}_{0}(u):=\sum_{v\in N_{u}^{+}\cap V_{0}}\tilde{d}_{uv}+\sum_{v\in N_{u}^{-}\cap V_{0}}(1-\tilde{d}_{uv}).

See
4

Claim 25
.

For
t=\nicefrac{{r}}{{2\delta}}
, it is the case that

E_{1a}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]\leq O(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}})\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

Proof of Claim
25
.

In this case we may upper bound
|E_{1}(u)|
by
|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|
. Applying Claim
4
, we obtain:

\displaystyle E_{1a}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|\Big]\leq 5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\Big]

\displaystyle=5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset\}}\Big|S_{d},S_{r}\Big]\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{1}_{\{\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)=\emptyset\}}\mid S_{d},S_{r}]\Big]

\displaystyle=5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot(1-q(\varepsilon))^{|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(u,t)|}\Big]

\displaystyle=\nicefrac{{10}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\Big]=\nicefrac{{10}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\nicefrac{{2128}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}=\nicefrac{{21280}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

In the second line, the outer expectation is over
S_{d}
and
S_{r}
while the inner is over
S_{p}
. Then in the second to last line, we use Corollary
2
, and in the last line we use Corollary
1
.
∎

Bounding
E_{1b}
.

For each
u\in V_{0}
with
\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset
, choose a fixed but arbitrary
z(u)\in\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)
. Note that because
z(u)\in S_{p}
and
\tilde{d}_{u,z(u)}\leq t\leq c\cdot r
, we know that
z(u)
is a
candidate
for clustering
u
, so in particular
s^{*}(u)
exists.
Note
z(u)
is a random variable depending on
S_{p},S_{d},
and
S_{r}
, but
not
on
S_{b}
.

Define

B(u):=\bigcup_{v\in E_{1}(u)}\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(s^{*}(v),r),

that is,
B(u)
is the union of balls in
S_{b}
, cut out around the vertices that cluster the vertices in
E_{1}(u)
.

We repeat the following three claims from the proof of Lemma
6
.

See
6

See
7

See
8

Claim 26
.

For
t=\nicefrac{{r}}{{2\delta}}
, it is the case that

E_{1b}:=\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\Big]\leq O(\nicefrac{{1}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}})\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

The following proof will look a bit different from the analogous claim in the proof of Lemma
6
, Claim
9
. There, we split
E_{1b}
based on whether
|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|
was above or below a threshold of

. Here we will need to be less lossy.

Proof of Claim
26
.

We split the sum based on whether or not the ball around
u
’s candidate cluster center
z(u)
, restricted to points in
S_{b}
, is non-empty. (Note this ball may be empty because
z(u)
is not necessarily in
S_{b}
.) We then apply Claim
6
.

\displaystyle E_{1b}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\geq 1\}}\Big]

(34)

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|=0\}}\Big]

(35)

Define
\frac{1}{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\geq 1\}}
to be 0 when
|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|=0
. Then we upper bound the quantity in line (
34
):

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)}|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\geq 1\}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)}8\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\frac{1}{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\geq 1\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)}8\cdot\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{1}{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|}\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\geq 1\}}\Big|S_{d},S_{r},S_{p}\Big]\Bigg].

In the second line we have applied Claims
7
and
8
. In the last line we have pulled out known factors. Finally, applying Lemma
5

with
A=|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|
,
S_{*}=S_{b}
, and
\mathcal{B}=\{S_{d},S_{r},S_{p}\}
, we continue upper bounding (
34
).

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\frac{112}{\varepsilon^{2}\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|}\Big]

\displaystyle=\nicefrac{{112}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\Big]=\nicefrac{{112}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\nicefrac{{2128}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}=\nicefrac{{238336}}{{\varepsilon^{6}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}}},

where in the last line we have used Corollary
1
.

Finally, we upper bound the quantity in line (
35
):

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}|E_{1}(u)|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|=0\}}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq 5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|=0\}}\Big]

\displaystyle=5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)\neq\emptyset}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathbf{1}_{\{|\text{Ball}^{S_{b}}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|=0\}}\Big|S_{d},S_{r},S_{p}\Big]\Bigg]

\displaystyle=5\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}:\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}^{S_{p}}(u,t)}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\cdot|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|\cdot(1-q(\varepsilon))^{|\text{Ball}_{\tilde{d}}(z(u),r)|}\Big]

\displaystyle=\nicefrac{{10}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}\Big[\sum_{u\in V_{0}}\tilde{D}_{0}(u)\Big]=\nicefrac{{10}}{{\varepsilon^{2}}}\cdot\nicefrac{{2128}}{{\varepsilon^{4}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}=\nicefrac{{21280}}{{\varepsilon^{6}}}\cdot\textsf{OPT}_{1}.

In the first line we have used Claims
4
and
6
. In the third line, we have taken out known quantities, as
the outer expectation is over
S_{d},S_{r},S_{p}
and the inner expectation is over
S_{b}
. Then in the penultimate line we have used Corollary
2
, and in the last line we have used Corollary
1
.

Combining the upper bounds on (
34
) and (
35
) finishes the claim.

∎

Combining the upper bounds in line (
33
) and Claims

and
